Theorem <a href="#thm:factorization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:factorization">1</a> uses the cited theorem to supply the same input without that assumption.

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The proof separates auxiliary-prime existence from the algebraic reduction. The dashed implication uses the cited analytic theorem; the remaining constructions are proved in the paper. The two middle branches are the effective and geometric parts of the odd-degree splitter.",
      "figure_id": "fig:route",
      "pdf_page": 6,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:route",
          "exact_quote": "The proof separates auxiliary-prime existence from the algebraic\nreduction. The dashed implication uses the cited analytic theorem;\nthe remaining constructions are proved in the paper. The two middle\nbranches are the effective and geometric",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[font=\\small,>=Latex,\n box/.style={draw,rounded corners=2pt,align=center,text width=5.2cm,\n minimum height=11mm,inner sep=5pt},node distance=9mm and 12mm]\n \\node[box,dashed] (analytic) {Uniform Hecke zero-free theorem\\\\from the companion};\n \\node[box,below=of analytic] (primes) {Polynomially bounded auxiliary primes};\n \\node[box,below=of primes] (table) {Finite fields and\\\\primary generators};\n \\node[box,below left=12mm and -12mm of table] (norm)\n   {Norm equations and\\\\divisor-class division};\n \\node[box,below right=12mm and -12mm of table] (geometry)\n   {Rational lifts and the\\\\infinity-lattice obstruction};\n \\node[box,below=28mm of table] (split) {A factor of a\\\\totally split polynomial};\n \\node[box,below=of split] (factor) {Complete factorization\\\\with multiplicities};\n \\draw[->,dashed] (analytic)--(primes);\n \\draw[->] (primes)--(table);\n \\draw[->] (table)--(norm);\n \\draw[->] (table)--(geometry);\n \\draw[->] (norm)--(split);\n \\draw[->] (geometry)--(split);\n \\draw[->] (split)--(factor);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A vertical flowchart with 6 levels:\n1. Top dashed rectangular box labeled \"Uniform Hecke zero-free theorem\\nfrom the companion\" connects via a downward dashed arrow to:\n2. Solid rectangular box labeled \"Polynomially bounded\\nauxiliary primes\", connecting via a downward solid arrow to:\n3. Solid rectangular box labeled \"Finite fields and\\nprimary generators\", which branches via two downward solid arrows to:\n4. Two side-by-side solid rectangular boxes: left labeled \"Norm equations and\\ndivisor-class division\" and right labeled \"Rational lifts and the\\ninfinity-lattice obstruction\". Both point via downward solid arrows to:\n5. Solid rectangular box labeled \"A factor of a\\ntotally split polynomial\", which connects via a downward solid arrow to:\n6. Bottom solid rectangular box labeled \"Complete factorization\\nwith multiplicities\"."
    },
    {
      "caption": "The two chains in Proposition 13. Tests run from left to right. Reaching test $t$ requires $λd_{t} = [W_{t}]$; equal labels at $t",
      "figure_id": "geom:ladder",
      "pdf_page": 19,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "geom:ladder",
          "exact_quote": "The two chains in Proposition~\\ref{geom:separation}.\nTests run from left to right.  Reaching test $t$ requires\n$\\lambda d_t=[W_t]$; equal labels at $t<m$ give\n$d_t=[W_{t+1}]$ and allow the next test.  Equal labels at the final test\nwould re",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Stealth, every node/.style={font=\\small},\n                    point/.style={minimum width=1.6cm,minimum height=0.7cm}]\n \\node at (-1.5,0) {$J$};\n \\node at (-1.5,-1.5) {$\\Lambda$};\n \\node[point] (d1) at (0,0) {$d_1$};\n \\node[point] (d2) at (2.6,0) {$d_2$};\n \\node[point] (dd) at (5.2,0) {$\\cdots$};\n \\node[point] (dm) at (7.8,0) {$d_m$};\n \\draw[->] (d2) -- node[above] {$\\lambda$} (d1);\n \\draw[->] (dd) -- node[above] {$\\lambda$} (d2);\n \\draw[->] (dm) -- node[above] {$\\lambda$} (dd);\n \\node[point] (w1) at (0,-1.5) {$W_1=Ne$};\n \\node[point] (w2) at (2.6,-1.5) {$W_2$};\n \\node[point] (ww) at (5.2,-1.5) {$\\cdots$};\n \\node[point] (wm) at (7.8,-1.5) {$W_m$};\n \\draw[->] (w1) -- node[above] {divide by $\\lambda$} (w2);\n \\draw[->] (w2) -- node[above] {divide by $\\lambda$} (ww);\n \\draw[->] (ww) -- node[above] {divide by $\\lambda$} (wm);\n \\node[below=1mm of wm] {$W_m\\notin\\lambda\\Lambda$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A two-row progression diagram showing:\n- Top row labeled \"$J$\": elements $d_1 \\xrightarrow{\\lambda} d_2 \\xrightarrow{\\lambda} \\cdots \\xrightarrow{\\lambda} d_m$.\n- Bottom row labeled \"$\\Lambda$\": elements $W_1 = Ne \\xrightarrow{\\text{divide by } \\lambda} W_2 \\xrightarrow{\\text{divide by } \\lambda} \\cdots \\xrightarrow{\\text{divide by } \\lambda} W_m$.\n- Below $W_m$ is an exclusion condition: \"$W_m \\notin \\lambda\\Lambda$\"."
    },
    {
      "caption": "$$\\mathcal B\\sim \\begin{pmatrix} *&*&*\\\\0&*&*\\\\0&0&*\\end{pmatrix} \\quad\\xrightarrow{\\ \\ker\\operatorname{tr}(ab)\\ }\\quad \\mathcal N\\sim \\begin{pmatrix}0&*&*\\\\0&0&*\\\\0&0&0\\end{pmatrix}, \\qquad U\\sim\\begin{pmatrix}k^{r_1}\\\\0\\\\0\\end{pmatrix}.$$ The fiber calculation with three distinct degree blocks. Each star denotes an arbitrary matrix block, and the diagonal block sizes are $r_{1}, r_{2}, r_{3}$. The common kernel $U$ of the trace-pairing kernel is the first block column space. Every matrix $vw$ with $v ∈ U$ belongs to $ℬ$; choosing $wv = 1$ gives the rank-one idempotent. The calculation works in the original coordinates.",

An arrow labeled \"$\\ker \\operatorname{tr}(ab)$\" pointing to matrix $\\mathcal{N} \\sim \\begin{pmatrix} 0 & * & * \\\\ 0 & 0 & * \\\\ 0 & 0 & 0 \\end{pmatrix}$ representing strictly block upper triangular matrices with zero diagonal blocks.\n3. Subspace vector representation $U \\sim \\begin{pmatrix} k^{r_1} \\\\ 0 \\\\ 0 \\end{pmatrix}$ showing a column vector whose top component is $k^{r_1}$ and remaining components are $0$."
